````text
## 你要做的
写 `tools\ball_cv.py`（一个脚本，`--help` 可跑），实现并**实测**至少这些手段的组合，挑出可用的：
1. **运动检测**：由于只有 25 fps，球滚动/飞行时帧差在球位置会留下小亮点；但也可能是纯旋转相机（转播机位会跟拍）⇒ 必须处理全局运动（相位相关或特征点估计全局平移/仿射，再做残差）。
2. **外观先验**：球是高亮度、低饱和度、近似圆的小 blob ⇒ 用 HSV 阈值 + 形态学 + 连通域，按面积/圆度/长宽比筛选。注意**白线、白色球衣、广告牌、观众席亮点**都是干扰项，必须用它们做调试。
3. **候选时空关联**：在短片段（例如 ±2 秒 = ±50 帧）里把逐帧候选串成轨迹，按「速度连续、加速度小、不瞬移」筛选，输出轨迹而不是单帧点。

- **不要修改** `.gitignore`、`docs/`、`src/`、`ai/`、`tools/check_repo.py`、`tools/` 下已存在的其它脚本、`data/samples/**`。
````

Important design detail for the "判据必须先能失败" requirement: the main criterion needs to be able to fail. The zero/static baseline: a detector that does nothing produces no trajectories → 0/10 pass. But that's trivially fail. Better: a meaningful baseline is "static appearance-only detector with no motion, and trajectory built from raw per-frame candidates without continuity filter" — that would pass a lot if there are many candidates (white lines, jerseys). Actually the shuffle control addresses that: if you build tracks from shuffled frames and still get ≥10-length smooth tracks, your continuity criterion is worthless.

Hmm, but shuffling frames means the "trajectory" is built over shuffled sequence — if the track builder allows large jumps, it will still form long tracks. So shuffle control measures discriminating power.

Also need a "static baseline": maybe a detector that ignores temporal information entirely, i.e., per-frame candidate at the same coordinates... Let me define:
- Baseline A ("no processing / static"): detector returns no candidates (or a fixed center pixel). Pass rate = 0.

Let me define baselines concretely:
1. `null` baseline: no candidate at all → 0/10 (trivially fails, proves the metric isn't auto-passing).
2. `static` baseline: a fixed pixel location (e.g. image center) as candidate for every frame → track of length 51, displacement 0, perfectly smooth → would PASS 10/10. This is the key "must be able to fail" control: a completely dumb static detector passes the length+smoothness criterion! Now the algorithm. Let me think carefully about what's likely to work at all.

Input: 101 frames 1280×720 @25fps from a broadcast. Camera pans (often fast), zooms.

Step 1: global motion. Use phase correlation on grayscale band-pass? Camera pan of whole image → phase correlation gives shift. But broadcast also has overlays (scoreboard) that are static in image while content moves — these corrupt global estimate. Mask out overlays? Could compute phase corr on the central region only.

Better: estimate global translation via phase correlation on a downsampled (e.g. 320x180) grayscale with a Hanning window, maybe restricting to a region. Also handle zoom via log-polar or just accept translation-only.

Now the algorithm details.

### Global motion estimation
Aspect: broadcast camera pans/tilts/zooms. For 41 ms frames, motion is small (a few px to maybe 20px during fast pans). Approach: 
1. Downscale to gray at 640x360 (or 320x180 for speed), apply Gaussian blur σ=1.
2. Phase correlation (cv2.phaseCorrelate with Hanning window) → shift (dx, dy) of current vs previous.
3. Optionally refine with ECC (cv2.findTransformECC with MOTION_EUCLIDEAN) at 640x360, starting from phase-corr estimate — but ECC is slow-ish (~30ms per frame at 640x360, fine for 1010 frames? 1010*50ms = 50s, acceptable).
4. Zoom handling: ECC MOTION_EUCLIDEAN includes rotation... MOTION_AFFINE would handle zoom. Use MOTION_AFFINE with a few iterations? Risky but let's try ECC with MOTION_EUCLIDEAN then MOTION_AFFINE.

Simpler and robust: estimate affine with ORB features + RANSAC. Grass has few corners but pitch lines and players do. But players move → outliers, RANSAC should cope if the majority is background.

I'll implement: primary = phase correlation (translation) on blurred gray at half res; secondary refinement = ECC affine on half res with the phase-corr result as init, gated by correlation coefficient; if ECC fails, keep translation. Then warp prev at full res with the affine (scaled 2x) and diff.

Overlays: scoreboard/logo static in image while scene moves → they'd appear as motion. Mitigation: after computing residual, ignore a border region? No — better: compute residual but also require appearance support; overlay text is white and would pass appearance. Hmm. Risk of track on the scoreboard logo. Mitigation: mask the static-overlay region. How to find it? Overlays are static in *image* coordinates → their difference over long time is low while scene changes. Actually a simple approach: compute per-pixel temporal median of frame differences... Overkill.

Alternative: use a "temporal variance" map over the clip: overlay pixels have low variance (static), grass has high variance under pan. Then mask out low-variance-but-bright regions? Grass under pan also changes a lot. Overlay region: nearly constant → variance ≈ compression noise. So mask = (var < small) & ... Actually grass region under pure pan changes a lot per pixel. So low-variance pixels are likely logo/scoreboard/graphic. But a static camera (no pan) would make everything low variance. Gate: apply overlay mask only where global motion magnitude is large? Getting complex.

Practical compromise: restrict analysis to a central ROI (e.g. the inner 80% width, from y=0.15H to 0.95H) — broadcast scoreboards are top-left/top-center, logos bottom. Plus compute and report per-frame which region candidates came from. I'll include an optional mask: `--roi` default 0.02..0.98 horizontally, 0.10..0.98 vertically, and note overlays are often inside. Hmm, scoreboard at top-left is inside the ROI.

Let me instead detect static-overlay mask by "pixelwise median of |frame_i - frame_0| is ~0 AND region is bright": simpler robust rule — compute M = median over time of the frame in absolute image coords... no.

OK: use temporal STD map S (per pixel, over sampled frames at 1/5 rate). Overlay = S < 6 AND mean brightness > 120? Under a panning camera grass S is also high, yes. Under a static camera, the whole scene has low S except moving stuff, so this mask would wrongly mask the field — but if the camera is static, then nothing is an overlay problem because the "static" scene legitimately doesn't move; the ball still moves and produces residual. Hmm but the mask would kill the ball's pixel region too (ball is small, mostly moving, so S high). Actually if camera static, ball moving → its position pixels have high S only in the frame when present. S is per-pixel temporal std over 20 samples: a pixel the ball passes through has high variance briefly → included. OK-ish.

Simplest: skip overlay masking, but detect and report it as a known failure mode by checking if tracks land on the same image coordinate across many clips (would be a red flag). And I can visually inspect.

Given time budget, let me prioritize: implement clean pipeline with translation+affine global motion, residual, appearance, association, controls, viz. Then measure.

### Motion residual
- gray full res, GaussianBlur (5,5) or σ=1.2
- warped_prev = warpAffine(prev_gray, M)
- d = |cur - warped_prev|
- also compute "max of forward/backward" to be robust? Use symmetric: for each frame i, residual_i = min(d(i-1→i), d(i→i+1))? A true ball gives high d in both (appears at new position in both). Noise at edges gives high in one direction maybe. Actually use min of |I_t - warp(I_{t-1})| and |I_t - warp(I_{t+1})| — a moving bright dot is bright in I_t and absent in both neighbors at that pixel → both diffs high. A compression artifact is inconsistent. Hmm, min reduces recall for the ball too (if motion is small, warp(prev) at the ball position contains the ball → low diff). Since ball moves fast, fine. I'll compute both and use `dmotion = np.minimum(dprev, dnext)` as the "consistent" motion map and record `Dprev/Dnext` separately.

Then threshold at e.g. > 18 (8-bit gray). Then connected components with area 4..300, and bbox <= 24px.

### Appearance
HSV on full res, slight blur:
- white-ish: S < 70, V > 140. Also allow slightly yellowish (V high, S up to 90).
- connected components in that mask with area 4..300, bbox w,h in [2,20], aspect 0.5..2.0, fill ratio ≥ 0.45, circularity ≥ 0.55.
- On green grass, the white-lines are thin long: excluded by aspect/size mostly, but a line segment crossing could be 20x6. Add: reject if the component's bbox extends beyond 20px in either dimension.

The white-line problem: line pixels are white → each connected component along a line is long (not small). But if the line is broken by compression, small pieces appear. That's a known false-positive source, and the track will follow the line while the camera pans (the line moves with the scene!) → smooth motion. This is *the* fake-green risk: a track on a white line will be perfectly smooth because it moves with the global motion. I can measure that: the ball should have motion mostly *not* equal to the global motion (ball moves independently, fast). New discriminator: **global-motion-relative motion** — the track's per-frame displacement should not equal the global background shift... but a ball moving exactly like background (camera tracking a pass) is possible. Still, a strong discriminator for the static-white-line case: the residual support (dmotion) must be positive; a line under pan has zero residual after compensation (it moves with the scene). So requiring motion residual support kills line tracks. 

That's exactly why I combine: candidate must have appearance (white blob) AND motion residual (moved relative to the pitch). Line pixels: appearance yes, motion residual no. Players: motion residual yes, but size big. Good combination.

### Association
Per frame candidates with score = w1*app + w2*motion. Associate greedily/DP over frames with constant velocity prediction, gate: dist ≤ max_speed*dt where max_speed = 45 px/frame at 720p? Ball speed in image can be high during a long pass: a 60 px/frame would be a rocket. Let's set gate 40 px and speed change (accel) ≤ 20 px/frame². Hmm: "no jump exceeding 3x ball diameter" = 24 px with D=8. But that's a criterion for smoothness, and with 25fps and a fast ball, 24px/frame is plausible for a shot. The criterion as given is what we must evaluate. I'll implement MAX_STEP = 3*D_px where D_px configurable default 8 → 24 px. And report max step.

Note: with a max step of 24 px, association gate 26 px.

Track building: multi-frame DP/greedy. Simple approach: 
- For each frame t and candidate c, maintain tracks; greedy: for each active track, predict pos via last velocity, choose nearest candidate within gate; if none, allow 1-2 frame gap (occlusion) then drop. This is standard. Tracks must have ≥10 detections covering ≥10 frames and near-continuous (gaps ≤2).

Better: exhaustive "beam search": keep top-K tracks per end frame (like a Viterbi). With few candidates per frame (hopefully <20), a beam search with K=200 is cheap and better than greedy. I'll do DP over candidate nodes: for each node, best predecessor within gate minimizing (accel penalty + 1 - score). That's O(N^2 * frames) with N ~ 20 → trivial. Then backtrack the best chains and enforce length ≥10 and max step.

Ugh, but the DP allows chains that jump around as long as within gate. The smoothness constraint requires |v_t - v_{t-1}| small → in DP, cost includes accel term; and a hard gate on accel (e.g. ≤ 12 px/frame change). I'll enforce hard accel gate.

### Controls
- `null`: no candidates → 0/10 pass.
- `static_center`: candidate = image center each frame, score forced 1 → 1 track, 101 frames, zero displacement → pass 10/10. This is the "static baseline" showing criterion has no discriminative power alone.
- `shuffled`: permute frame indices of the clip (destroys true temporal order) then run the same pipeline (motion computed on shuffled sequence is meaningless but the pipeline doesn't know) → pass rate. If similar to real, continuity criterion not discriminative. 
  Note shuffling with global motion estimation will produce huge "global motion" values; the residual will be noise. Might fail naturally. To be a fair control I should keep the same detector: shuffle the frame order, treat order as time. Report.
- Also `appearance_only` (no motion residual term) and `motion_only` (no appearance) ablations would be very informative for "which手段可用". The task says "实现并实测至少这些手段的组合，挑出可用的" → ablation is exactly the way to "挑出可用的". Let me add ablation variants: full, no_motion, no_appearance. Running 3 variants × 10 clips is 3× cost. Cost per clip: 151 frames extraction (fast, 0.4s) + processing 101 frames. Processing: full-res gray ops ~5ms, ECC ~40ms → ~5s per clip. 10 clips → 50s per variant. Fine. Extraction 10 × 0.6s.

Plus shuffled control: 10 clips × maybe 3 shuffles = 30 more runs → 150s. Acceptable but let me use 1 shuffle per clip with a fixed seed (and report). Maybe 2 seeds. Let's see runtime.

Additional control idea: since we don't have ground truth, a strong negative control is "does the track point at something that is actually the ball?" — visual. Plus a positive control: does the detector find the ball in a synthetic case? I could add a *synthetic positive control*: take a test frame, composite a synthetic 8px white disk moving smoothly across it, and check the detector recovers the track. That is an excellent "can it fail" test — it proves the pipeline works when a ball-like object exists, distinguishing "algorithm broken" from "ball invisible". Very valuable for the report. I'll add `--synthetic` control: for each clip's frames, draw a moving 8px anti-aliased white disk with slight blur (simulating compression), run the same pipeline (skipping the extraction), and measure whether a ≥10-frame track covers the center frame and passes near the ground truth position (e.g. center within 3 px). That gives a "ceiling" measurement.

That's a strong evaluation design:
1. null baseline → 0
2. static center → 10/10 (proves criterion alone is worthless)
3. shuffled → x/10
4. synthetic ball injected → y/10 (upper bound of the algorithm; if y=10/10 the detector works, so any 0/10 on real is due to the ball's actual visibility, not a bug)
5. real → z/10

And report "if z≈0 while y=10, conclusion = 传统 CV 在这批画面上不可行 / 需要条件".

Details on candidate extraction:

```python
def detect_candidates(bgr, motion, cfg):
    hsv = cv2.cvtColor(bgr, cv2.COLOR_BGR2HSV)
    H,S,V = split
    white = ((S <= cfg.sat_max) & (V >= cfg.val_min)).astype(np.uint8)
    # clean
    white = cv2.morphologyEx(white, cv2.MORPH_OPEN, np.ones((2,2),np.uint8))  # remove 1px specks
    n, lab, stats, cent = cv2.connectedComponentsWithStats(white, 8)
    app = []
    for i in 1..n:
        x,y,w,h,area = stats[i]
        if not (cfg.area_min <= area <= cfg.area_max): continue
        if w > cfg.max_wh or h > cfg.max_wh: continue
        ar = w/h ; if not (1/cfg.aspect_max <= ar <= cfg.aspect_max)
        fill = area/(w*h) ; if fill < cfg.fill_min
        # circularity via contour
        ...
        # motion support: mean of motion inside the component
        m = motion[lab==i]  -> mean, max
        score ...
```
vectorizing connectedComponentsWithStats is fine.

Circularity: compute per-component contour only for components in size range (few) → cv2.findContours on the mask restricted... Simpler: circularity ≈ 4πA/P² needs perimeter. Alternative cheap proxy: fill ratio in bbox for a disk = π/4 ≈ 0.785; a square 1.0; a line fragment elongated. Combined with aspect ratio it's adequate. I'll add "roundness" = area / (π*(max(w,h)/2)^2) which is ~1 for a disk, lower for a line. Use extent and roundness instead of contour perimeter — cheaper and no contour API issues.

For the ball at 4-8 px on grass, the white mask may only catch the bright core (2-3 px) → area 4-9. Set area_min = 3, area_max = 250 (a 12px ball → ~113 px at D=12). Also allow the "dim white" ball (compressed) — S may be < 90, V > 120.

Motion map: after warping, dmotion = min(dprev, dnext) which is 0 where no motion. Candidates need motion support > thr (e.g. 12). But some ball pixels may be static-ish... For "no_motion" ablation I ignore this.

Also candidates from motion-only: strong motion blobs that don't pass appearance (useful ablation).

Track scoring: 
- cost for node = -(app_score_normalized) 
- DP with hard gates.
Return list of tracks with per-frame positions, scores, max step, mean score.

Evaluation for a clip:
- tracks = build(...)
- passing = [t for t in tracks if len(t)>=TRACK_MIN_LEN and max_step<=MAX_STEP and covers center frame]
- clip pass if any.

Then "per-frame report" = each test frame = one clip.

```python
def build_tracks(cands, n_frames, cfg):
    # cands: list per frame of dict(x,y,score,app,mot)
    # DP: best_prev[i] index into frame t-1 nodes
    nodes = []  # list of lists
    back = []
    for t in range(n_frames):
        cur = cands[t]
        for c in cur: c['id']=...
        if t==0 or not prev:
            for c in cur: c['dp_score']=c['score']; c['prev']=-1; c['length']=1
        else:
            for c in cur:
                best=None
                for j,p in enumerate(prev):
                    d = dist(c,p); 
                    if d > gate: continue
                    # velocity continuity
                    if p['prev']>=0:
                        pp = prev[p['prev']]... 
```
Hmm carrying velocity chain: use p's own velocity computed from its predecessor position. Store per node its predecessor index (into previous frame's node list) so we can compute.

But a node's dp_score depends on the chain; greedy per node over predecessors ignoring velocity history may allow a kink. Enforce accel: for each (c,p) pair, if p has prev, compute v_prev = p.pos - prev_p.pos, v_new = c.pos - p.pos; require |v_new - v_prev| ≤ accel_max. That's a local 2nd-order constraint — good enough and keeps DP O(N²).

dp_score = p.dp_score + c.score - dist_penalty(d)... Actually score in [0,1]; use reward = c.score - 0.02*d - 0.05*accel. Keep max. length = p.length+1.

```python
def _components(mask, area_min, area_max, max_wh, aspect_max, extent_min, round_min):
    n, lab, st, cen = cv2.connectedComponentsWithStats(mask, 8, cv2.CV_32S)
    out = []
    for i in range(1, n):
        x, y, w, h, area = int(st[i,0]), int(st[i,1]), int(st[i,2]), int(st[i,3]), int(st[i,4])
        ...
    return out, lab
```

Also unused variables: `best_e` referenced before assignment in estimate_motion if the phase shift fails; and the loop logic is convoluted. Let me rewrite estimate_motion cleanly:

```python
def estimate_motion(prev_s, cur_s, allow_affine=True):
    h, w = cur_s.shape
    best_e = _mae(prev_s, cur_s)  # identity
    M = identity; kind="identity"; cc=0.0
    sh, resp = phase_shift(prev_s, cur_s)
    if valid:
        for cand in (Mt, Mt_inv):
            e = mae(warp(prev_s,cand), cur_s)
            if e < best_e: best_e, M, kind, cc = e, cand, "trans", resp
    if allow_affine:
        try:
            init = M
            cc2, Mw = cv2.findTransformECC(prev_s, cur_s, init, MOTION_AFFINE, crit)
            for cand in (Mw, invert(Mw)):
                e = mae(warp(prev_s,cand), cur_s)
                if e < best_e * 1.0: best_e, M, kind, cc = e, cand, "affine", cc2
        except: pass
    return M, kind, cc
```
Note: findTransformECC modifies warpMatrix in place; must pass a copy. Also ECC requires the warp to be CV_32F and 'inputImage' same size as template.

Hmm, one gotcha: ECC with MOTION_AFFINE from an init that's the identity/translation and a scene with big pan might diverge; but cc>0.3 gate protects.

Now rewrite detect_candidates with: integral-image ring contrast, top-K, motion as a score bonus, no hard motion gate in full mode.

Design:
- appearance blobs from white mask; filters: area, wh, aspect, extent, roundness; compute ring contrast; require ring >= RING_MIN (this is a real filter: ball 45-58, ads up to 90 → doesn't exclude ads but excludes blobs in bright neighborhoods, e.g. inside billboards/crowd whites). Then score.
- mot from dmot: peak inside blob mask.
- full: score = 0.30*bright + 0.15*round + 0.15*ext + 0.25*ring + 0.15*mot
- no_motion: drop mot term, renormalize: 0.35*bright+0.18*round+0.17*ext+0.30*ring
- no_appearance: motion blobs only, score = 0.6*clip(peak/60) + 0.4*clip(area match) ... keep simple: peak/60 clipped, plus roundness bonus.
- top-K truncation by score at the end (applies to all modes).

Ring contrast via integral image: ii over V (float64). rect(x0,y0,x1,y1) exclusive. inner = blob bbox; outer = bbox padded by pad = max(3, max(w,h)) but clamp. contrast = inner_mean - (outer_sum - inner_sum)/(outer_area - inner_area).

But inner should be the blob mask mean rather than bbox mean; bbox is fine and simpler, and the blob fills ≥50% of bbox.

Let me restructure main:
```
files = extract_clip(video, r["t"], args.extract_pad, cache, name, force)
idx, mae, maes = locate_center(files, ref)
t_hit = r["t"] - args.extract_pad + idx/FPS
# 再抽一次，以 t_hit 为中心，保证 ±half 窗口完整
if idx/FPS - args.half < 0.2 or (len(files)-idx)/FPS - args.half < 0.2:
    cache2 = CACHE_DIR / (name + "_c")
    files2 = extract_clip(video, t_hit, args.half + 0.6, cache2, name, force=True)
    idx2, mae2, _ = locate_center(files2, ref)
    files, idx, mae = files2, idx2, mae2
    t_hit = t_hit - (args.half+0.6) + idx2/FPS
```
Then t_matched = t_hit (recomputed). And r["t_offset"] = t_hit - r["t"].

Note the second extraction's ffmpeg seeks to t_hit-2.6 → keyframe seeking should be frame-accurate with -ss before -i? Actually with `-ss` before `-i`, ffmpeg seeks and then decodes; it's accurate since 2.1 (it seeks to the keyframe then decodes forward). Should be fine — the MAE match will confirm.

```python
            bright = min(1.0, max(0.0, (v_mean - VAL_MIN) / 70.0))
```

Problem: everything saturates at 1.0, and top-1 scores were exactly 1.000. Use soft-knee (tanh) instead of hard clip:
soft(x) = x / (1 + x) → in [0,1), monotone, no ties.

bright_s = bright/(1+bright) etc. But then differences compress. Alternative: keep linear, no cap: score = 0.45*app + ... with components in raw normalized units; magnitudes ~1.0-1.5. Ties unlikely. Motion term must not dominate: mot/60 with peak 130 → 2.17 → 0.3*2.17 = 0.65 vs app ~0.5. Hmm, motion would dominate. Use sqrt compression: mot_s = (mot_peak/60)**0.5 → 1.47.

The static digit persists 100 frames → its accumulated val is huge because length dominates. That's the key algorithmic insight: sum of scores over 101 frames beats a 40-frame ball track. The tracker prefers long static chains, which is exactly the "zero-motion shortcut". 

This is a genuine, reportable failure mode: **静止高亮块（比分牌、广告牌、看台）比会动的球更容易形成长轨迹**，而判据只看长度和平滑度 ⇒ 判据系统性地奖励静止假目标。

And critically: this is also why static_center baseline "passes" — a fixed point is the perfect fake.

So: the primary criterion as specified (≥10 frames + smooth) is trivially satisfied by static blobs; a *moving* real ball is punished by comparison. That's a solid finding, and it argues the criterion must include "the track must move at a plausible ball speed OR be provably near the play".

Hmm, chain_len 18 out of 101 while the ball is present in all frames → the chain breaks. Because the DP's `prev` links are chosen to maximize val, not to follow the ball! The DP is a *global* optimization: at each frame, a node's prev is the best predecessor. For the ball's node at frame k, the best predecessor might be a *different* object (e.g., a static blob near its previous position) if that gives a higher val. So the chain through the ball's node is not necessarily the ball's path — the DP mixes objects. That's the "DP is a greedy max-val assignment" issue: it may switch targets, producing long-but-wrong chains.

So the ball's chain (as reconstructed by `chain()`) has length 18 and mean_score 2.86 — lower than the winner's 3.801 (51 frames of something else, e.g. a bright ad or a player).

Since the injected ball's chain is only 18 frames at mean 2.86 (val ~51) vs the real ball's chain 31 × 3.8 (val 115), the REAL ball (which is actually in the frame) wins. Hmm interesting: in match_004 the real ball's motion is stronger.

So the synthetic probe's failure here is: **the DP's val-optimum prefers whatever chain is longest×brightest, and the injected ball's own chain gets truncated because the DP reassigns its predecessor nodes to other objects**. The injected ball is not "sticky".

This is a genuine algorithmic weakness: the DP's node-level best-predecessor choice doesn't enforce that a chain corresponds to one object; and the injected ball (fast, 17 px/frame) may be lost because at each step the best predecessor is a nearby static blob with a higher accumulated val.

Also relevant: the injected ball's speed 11-18 px/frame vs the gate 26 — near the limit; combined with the accel gate 10 and my motion-warp gate, the ball's own chain may break because the candidate position jitters (the ball's centroid moves 17.6 px/frame; a jitter of ±5px is 28% of the step → accel ~5; ok).

Whatever: the *real* conclusion is more nuanced than "features are fine, ranking is broken". The honest summary:

Facts measured:
1. The appearance channel does produce a candidate at the ball in 8/9 frames of match_004 (median dist 2.0px) at rank 1-3 by score (full mode, median rank 1; no_motion rank median 6).
2. In match_004, with a val-based pick, the auto pick lands on the ball (seed_dist 1.5px, 31-frame chain, |v| 6.8 px/frame) — so for a *moving* ball in a static-camera clip, the pipeline can find it.
3. But over the 10 test frames, 5/10 winners are static clutter (watermark/ad/scoreboard) with mean_step<1: matches 020/024/028/036/040. 1/10 on the ball (004). Others: need my review of 008/012/016/032.
4. Motion channel alone (no_appearance) can't hit the ball (0/9, median dist 15.7px) — the ball's residual peak is not a compact blob at the ball center; the peak is at its edges/next to the player.
5. Injected 8px synthetic ball: enters the candidate set 9/10 (0.59px) and its chain passes M1 9/10, but the auto-pick never selects it (0/10, 378px away) because the DP's val-optimum prefers longer chains of static clutter. Note: match_004's real-ball chain (31 frames, val 115) actually beat the synthetic injected ball's chain (18 frames, val 51) in the same clip — i.e., even a *perfect* synthetic target loses to longer clutter chains.
6. shuffle 6/10 (vs real 10/10) → the continuity criterion has *some* discriminative power but 60% of shuffled clips still pass → weak.
7. static_center (no detection at all) 10/10 → M1 alone is trivial to pass.

So the honest verdict: **不可行 in the current form**, with a precise reason: even when the ball is a top-ranked candidate and can be chained, a global val-maximizing trajectory selector prefers long-lived static high-contrast clutter (watermark "独播", ad boards, scoreboard), and the pass criterion can't tell the difference; the only frame where the ball was picked is the one where the ball moved fast with a static camera and no competing static blob was adjacent.

That's a solid, honest, well-evidenced negative result. And I have a concrete improvement path: per-frame candidate ranking + ROI gating by players (the ball is always near a player's feet/hands in these frames), or a temporal "the target must be a *single* object" constraint, or a learned classifier on 40x40 crops.

Now the honest verdict for the *auto* pipeline (no seed): 1/10. And with the seed at the true ball position (match_004), the tracker recovers a 31-frame chain at 1.5px — i.e., **when the ball actually moves, the motion+appearance combination can follow it**, but nothing in the pipeline chooses it over the clutter.

Key quantified findings to report:
1. Ball candidate exists: 8/9 frames in match_004 within 2.0px (median), rank 1 (full) / rank 6 (appearance-only).
2. Motion channel alone: 0/9 within 6px → the ball's motion residual peak doesn't form a clean blob at the ball (it's the leading/lagging edge + it's next to the player's legs).
3. Auto pick: 1/10 on the ball; 5/10 winners are static (watermark/ads), 4/10 are moving clutter (boots, board edges). The pick rule (val = cumulative score) awards longevity, so a 101-frame watermark beats a 31-frame ball. When I used the "longest chain" rule the ball was 0/10; with the val rule it's 1/10 — both bad, and I explicitly measured both to avoid claiming the rule change fixed it.
4. synthetic injection (8px white disk, 11-18px/frame): enters candidates 9/10 (0.59px median), chain passes M1 9/10, but auto-pick never selects it (0/10) — even a *guaranteed-perfect* target loses to clutter because the DP's val accumulates over 101 frames of static clutter while the injected ball's chain breaks (its own nodes get re-linked to brighter neighbours).
   Wait — why does the injected ball's chain break at 18 frames? Let me explain honestly: `chain_len` 10-18 for most clips means the reconstructed chain through the ball's node covers only 10-18 frames. The cause: at each frame the DP reassigns the ball's node's predecessor to whichever node maximizes cumulative score; with a fast target it's frequently a nearby ad-board blob, so the chain "jumps off" the ball. I should verify this explanation rather than assert it... The simpler and defensible statement: "注入球自身那条链只有 10~18 帧（DP 在每个节点挑的是「累计得分最高的前驱」，快速目标很容易被旁边的静止亮块接走），而静止水印/广告是 101 帧 ⇒ val 差值压倒得分差。"

So the pipeline's failure has three components, all measured:
1. Candidate generation works (88-92% recall for an 8px injected ball; 8/9 for the real ball in match_004).

3. Trajectory selection: val = sum of scores over the chain ⇒ longevity dominates ⇒ 5/10 winners are 101-frame static clutter.

(a) clip `rngv` (and motv) into [0,1] so no single term dominates;
(b) penalize chain longevity: use mean_score (not val) to pick, among chains with length ≥ TRACK_MIN_LEN — because a ball chain of 31 frames with mean 3.8 should beat a watermark chain of 101 frames with mean 1.9.

Design: 
- `GRASS_ANN = (6, 11)`, `GRASS_ANN_MIN = 0.70`.
- In detect_candidates, if grass on: compute grass mask (no dilation), integral, then for each candidate compute annulus mean via integral of ring = mean(r<=R1) - mean(r<=R0) properly weighted: use integral over square? Easier: build a circular kernel mask approach: compute via cv2.filter2D with a disk kernel for r<=R1 and r<R0, then annulus mean = (sum1 - sum0)/(area1-area0). That's cheap and exact.
- Also apply to motion blobs.

Let me code it: at top of detect_candidates, if grass is not None: gr_ii... Actually simplest: 
```
if use_grass:
    gmask = grass_mask_raw(hsv)  # float32 0/1
    k1 = disk_kernel(R1); k0 = disk_kernel(R0-1)
    s1 = cv2.filter2D(gmask, -1, k1, borderType=cv2.BORDER_CONSTANT)
    s0 = cv2.filter2D(gmask, -1, k0, ...)
    ann = (s1 - s0) / (k1.sum() - k0.sum())
```
filter2D with normalized=False. Then index ann[y,x].

Border handling: border constant 0 is fine (frame edge → fewer grass → rejected; acceptable).

Cost: two filter2D on 1280x720 float32 — kernel 23x23, that's ~fast enough (few ms... maybe 20-30ms). Fine.
